(PL) Madam President, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and During the years of the PO-PSL coalition, more than half of the stations operating in Poland were eliminated. One of the demands of these meetings was the expectation that these posts would return. After all, when they were liquidated, we protested against the liquidation of the posts, protested as a Parliamentary Law and Justice Club, I protested as vice-president of the Parliamentary Committee on Administration and Home Affairs. Locals, locals protested, and police stations were being shut down, trying to implement the concept of the police on the phone. We have observed that this is a wrong concept, which has not worked anywhere and will not work in Poland either. According to our programme, the Law and Justice programme, which is currently the government programme of Prime Minister Beata Szydło, we are restoring the police stations. During the consultation 150 locations were reported, where the stations were expected to reactivate. After these consultations, after the first quarter of 2016, further needs are reported, for the time being 176 such places are reported in Poland. But you can predict it's not over. In 2016, actually in the second half of 2016, because then it started, you had to prepare for it. On a national scale, we've reactivated 37 stations. In 2017, 33 stations are scheduled to be restored and this figure may increase, also due to the logistical involvement of local authorities. Self-governments are generally interested in returning their stations to their municipalities and engage logistically, primarily by providing facilities in which these stations are re-located and by making a financial contribution related to the renovation of these facilities. Today we estimate the contribution of local governments at about 700,000. zł, when it comes to the stations that have been reactivated. And already this year, in January, two out of these 33 precincts have been restored to this day. Ladies and gentlemen! In order to keep the residents safe every day, the police must be close to the people. It has to be where normal everyday life takes place. Where people live, live, work, send their children to school, participate in sports and cultural events where they shop, go to church - there must be police. Not far, not a few dozen kilometers away and only responding to calls. Besides, the response time is shorter when the station is close, but more importantly, the recognition of threats is greater in a situation where the police operate on a daily basis near those places where normal everyday life takes place. So that the restoration of posts is not only a socially expected phenomenon, but also a desirable phenomenon when it comes to increasing the safety and security of Poles. According to recent studies, Poles have a high sense of security in their place of residence. Very high, because it reaches up to 95%, but right where the police are close, you can clearly see that this feeling is bigger. Therefore, this process will continue not only in 2017, but also in the following years. We assume that this process will continue by 2020, and we cannot conclude that any individual cases will not take place later. The important thing is that in many places, unfortunately, where the stations have been wound up, there are no buildings today where they can be re-localized, because those that previously served the stations have been used for other purposes, lent to someone else, sold, and there are also cases where they simply destroyed at that time and today require a lot of investment. Therefore, in the programme of modernization of uniformed services prepared by the Ministry of Home Affairs and Administration and adopted by the Government - in the programme already in force, because the law is signed by the President; this is the programme for the years 2017 So, in cooperation with the local authorities, and using these opportunities that I am referring to, we will strive to ensure that where the stations have been wound up, where there is an objective need to restore them, they can [[♪ The bell ♪]] to return and serve people, to serve the people, raising the level of security and security of citizens.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u11	Thank you very much, Minister. Now the question is asked by Mrs Anna Elżbieta Sobeck and Mr Wojciech Zubowski from the Law and Justice Club. You're welcome, my fellow Member. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u12	Thank you. Mr. Marshal! Minister! Legal information systems such as Lex or Legalis have been widely used for many years. They are used not only by law firms, but also by courts. Unfortunately, the Ministry of Justice does not allow courts to use more than one system. However, if a judge is to work efficiently and effectively, he must have adequate working conditions, which to a large extent consist of access to widely understood legal literature. A judge ruling in a district court must be given the opportunity to familiarise himself with a number of positions in order to apply substantive and procedural law correctly. Both Lex and Legalis provide access to other comments, magazines, literature or case law. The opinion of judges who have benefited from these two programmes for many years shows that they are indeed complementary to each other and not competitive. If access to comments, monographs, journals in electronic form is restricted, the work of a judge burdened with more and more responsibilities will deteriorate further. The above is therefore in favour of the need for judges to have access to both programmes in order to provide instruments for the best possible settlement of cases. Thank you.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u13	Thank you very much. Please respond to the Secretary of State at the Ministry of Justice of Minister Patrick Jaki. You're welcome.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u14	Thank you. Dear Marshal, Gentlemen of Ministers! High Seym! On 25 November 2016, the Ministry of Justice asked the President of the Public Procurement Office to give an opinion on the admissibility of the purchase of more than one legal information system for the determination of the procedure for the supply of legal information to the general courts for 2017. The procedure for the supply of software with a legal information system carried out by the Shopping Centre for the Judicial Tribunal in an open tender procedure has been launched in 2015 with a view to making a purchase for 2016 for all courts. On 30 November 2015, in the context of an appeal proceedings following an appeal by the contractor Beck sp. z o.o. The national Board of Appeal issued an unfavourable decision to the contracting authority under which the contracting authority had to significantly reduce the minimum requirements for the quantity of decisions and legal publication. This judgment was given despite the contracting authority's argument that the minimum requirements for the number of comments, decisions, publications and functionalities constitute legitimate needs of the courts. During the hearing the position was also presented that the reduction of the minimum requirements would not allow the purchasing needs of the contracting authority to be met. In view of the above, the contracting authority brought an action against the judgment of the National Board of Appeal at the District Court in Krakow on 21 December 2015. On 2 March 2016, that court delivered a judgment in which it failed to take account of the contracting party's complaint. In view of the above, the specifications of the essential terms of the contract have been amended accordingly, taking into account the settled decisions and amending the assumptions of the procedure, which currently covers the contract for 2017. The offers in the proceedings were submitted, first, by the consortium Beck sp. z o.o. with a gross price of PLN 2 869 388 and Wolters Kluwer SA with its registered office in Warsaw with a price of PLN 11 069 151.30. The price represented 50% of the evaluation criterion for tenders. Other points were offered for the content and functionality of the system. Following the evaluation of the tenders, including the exclusion of an abnormally low price for the most advantageous bid, the contracting authority selected the bid of the consortium Beck. The contractor Wolters Kluwer appealed against this decision, whose appeal was completely dismissed by the National Board of Appeal on 12 September. As a result, the contract for the supply of software with the legal information system was concluded with the consortium Beck. The procedure was conducted in an unlimited and competitive manner. At the same time, the contracting authority relied on the long-established position of the Public Procurement Office on the competitiveness of the legal information systems available on the market. The position of the Office in relation to the needs of the general courts shall be such as to permit the purchase of more than one system of legal information, even if such purchase appears justified on account of the needs of the general courts. The confirmed position of the President of the Public Procurement Office is, in principle, in line with the existing line of interpretation of the Authority concerning the competitiveness of legal information systems, according to which decisions have been taken in the past years in tendering procedures for the purchase of systems for universal courts. The Public Procurement Office once again pointed out that there is no basis for allowing the purchase of a system of legal information in the form of a free-of-charge contract and that the systems offered on the market are competitive with each other. According to the Authority, there is no monopoly on the market of the legal information system for one economic operator or one IT system and the programmes available are very similar. Similarly, although the content of both systems in part may be covered by exclusive rights, the fact that two economic operators are present on the market does not justify the use of the negotiated procedure. As regards the purchase of the legal information system for 2017, it does not appear possible to authorise the purchase of an additional system or to initiate a supplementary procedure in this respect. Nor will allowing the general courts to buy legal information systems on their own cause the problems to be eliminated. In addition to the high risk of breach of public procurement law, there may be a drastic increase in expenditure on the purchase of a system of legal information and further sanctioning the lack of competition on the publishing market. On the other hand, with regard to subsequent proceedings, the Public Procurement Office identified certain legal steps that could be taken in order to produce more than one legal information system and related to the amendment of the Public Procurement Law, which entered into force on 22 June 2016, e.g. by dividing the contract into parts, specifying in the specifications the essential terms of the contract the scope and subject matter of those parts and limiting at the same time the possibility for the contractor to submit a tender to only one part. It may be difficult to achieve one expected effect of purchasing more systems in this case without the risk of breaching public procurement rules, cost-effectiveness and fair competition. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u30	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Indeed, as of 1 January 2017, new rules have been introduced for the distribution of the basic grant to public universities. However, I would like to stress that the changes made to the distribution of the basic grant to public universities place, above all, a greater emphasis on the quality of education, the quality of research carried out and the internationalisation of universities. Of course, the availability of academic staff for students, measured by the number of students and PhD students per academic teacher, is important in the new allocation algorithm. Public universities supervised by the Minister responsible for higher education are characterized by a very wide variation of this parameter, according to our estimates - from 7 to 27. Even in groups of homogeneous universities, such as polytechnic universities or pedagogical universities, there is a significant difference in learning availability. Universities where the number of students and PhD students per academic teacher is below the reference level set out in the Regulation will have a proportion of the grants resulting from the student-doctoral component under the applicable rules. On the other hand, in those universities where this relationship, this learning accessibility rate will significantly exceed the reference level, the grant will be significantly lower, depending, of course, on the scale of the exceedance. Another key element of the allocation of the algorithmic basic grant is the introduction for universities - this change does not apply to professional universities - in the staff component of the indicator of scientific potential. This indicator is determined on the basis of the Law on the Financing of Science and amounts to A+ - 1,5 for higher education institutions with category A - 1 and for categories B and C - 0,7 respectively. and 0,4. These scales are estimated as averages for the entire university on the basis of the units forming the university concerned. The indicator of scientific potential through the award of universities, which combine high quality research with education, will have a positive impact on the quality of education in a given university. The research activities of academic teachers are taken into account in the categories of scientific units, the number of quotes and the number of projects carried out or international cooperation is taken into account. At the same time, I would like to stress that, due to the stability of the funding of universities and the guarantee of their financial liquidity, a fuse has been introduced that stops the level of subsidies, i.e. does not allow for increases and decreases of more than 5% per year. This gives universities sufficient time to prepare for new funding rules without the need for economically and socially unjustified action. This means that the grant for a given university from January 2017 will consist of 57% permanent transfer - completely independent of current university parameters - and 50% since 2018. The rest, on the other hand, is a variable part, with a reservation I mentioned that the grant cannot be reduced or increased by more than 5%. From year to year. With regard to more detailed explanations regarding the losses of small universities, of course, if we assume that small universities are state-owned vocational schools, then given that the algorithm is distributed - and this is not a new thing, because the previous one was also of such a nature - i.e. with a similar financial envelope only the division is changed, we have estimated on the basis of data from 2015, which are most recent or reliable at the present time, that 14 vocational universities will receive or can obtain a smaller grant than in the previous year and 25 vocational universities will receive a higher grant than in the previous year. In the +/ range, on the other hand? 2% We estimate that it will be okay. 11 universities, i.e. 28% all vocational universities. For comparison in the previous algorithmic breakdown of the basic grant, including in the +/ range? 2% There were 16 universities, much more than 30%. I would also like to stress that if we take the line of reasoning from the Member that there are regional universities, i.e. small and large, and not good teaching and poor teaching or good and poor science, I can say that there are universities such as the UMK in Toruń or the University of Warmia and Mazury in Olsztyn, or the Częstochow University of Technology - these are only some examples - which will be divided according to the new algorithm, but there are also large universities, if we are already applying this type of division, which may receive a smaller grant as a result of the division. With regard to regional universities, if we can put it this way, that is to say to the region that you represent, two vocational universities, according to initial simulations, may lose here in terms of grants, while three vocational universities, universities in Sanok, Krosnie or Przemyśl, will gain from a new algorithm. Thank you.

The total network of public roads in Poland is close to 420 thousand. km, and the national roads are less than 20,000. km, so less than 5% The whole network. True, national roads carry 60% traffic, however 95% these are self-government roads and the question about the state of local roads is most justified, especially since the NIK report, which described the state of local roads last year, actually showed them a very bad condition, showed the huge needs that are to improve this state of local roads. But maybe he's already referring to specific questions. As far as local government roads are concerned, as a government, we have two options for direct support for road construction on local roads. First of all, it is a programme of development of the municipal and district road infrastructure network for the years 2016-2019. 3800 million PLN is guaranteed for this. As far as this programme is concerned, there are five criteria that decide on the allocation of the resources requested by individual local authorities. This is primarily about municipal and district authorities. And specifically in the previous year, 2016, 800 million zlotys were distributed. In the first period, 492 tasks from individual municipalities and powiats were selected in the first applications, while a total of 850 tasks were carried out, of which 538 were carried out on municipal roads and 311 were carried out on district roads. For this year, 2017, the deadline for submitting applications was 15 September. They were considered and the letters were approved by 31 December. The limit available for this year is also 800 million PLN. The second possibility to finance or co-finance local roads is the provision for general subsidies. In 2016 335 million PLN was allocated for this. It has been used in its entirety, a total of 347 tasks have been carried out. The latter reserve is particularly suitable for the voivodship roads that you have been asking about, particularly as regards bridge objects, because one of the main objectives for which this reserve is operational is to support modernisation, to assist repairs or even sometimes to build bridge crossings. On the other hand, as regards the question of the possibility of retraining roads from voivodships to national roads, it is a possibility. The proposal in this case should be submitted by the Management Board of a particular voivodship to the Minister of Infrastructure and Construction or possibly to the Minister responsible for Transport. This proposal is then assessed by the General Director of National Roads and Motorways, and the decision must be made by the end of the third quarter of the year in order that from next year onwards, from 1 January, the road can change its qualification to the national road. This, of course, must be properly motivated, considered, because the point is not only to change the category, but only to make sure that the justification and change actually improve the coherence of the road network, to maintain it. But please bear in mind that together with such, only such mechanical transfer, even a decision, the resources of the general management are not increasing, but they simply need to be divided differently. Formally, this possibility exists, but the initiative lies here on the side of the management board of the voivodship. On the other hand, I would also like to say with regard to one of the proposals that you have put forward... Namely the circumstances of increasing expenditure on self-government roads. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u92	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! First, I wanted to thank the applicants sincerely for putting this problem up. There is also a steering committee appointed by the Prime Minister on 21 October 2016 by the Prime Minister on the conduct of these activities. Ladies and gentlemen! At the same time, I can say thank you that the situation is, in fact, worrying from the point of view of air quality, from the point of view of European standards, and society has a right to be interested in this. The environmental inspection carried out audits for 2015 in accordance with its tasks, as regards 2016, is now a summary. This check showed that 46 zones in Poland in 39 exceeded PM10 suspended dust standards, 23 PM2.5, and for the next element of the atmosphere that we monitor - benzopyren - up to 44. So, indeed, the situation can be disturbing. At this point, we need to be clear about what this pollination depends on. So it has an impact on this, and there is no doubt about it, a matter of fuel quality, and these fuels are a matter of their quality. Of course, emissions depend on what we smoke, and this is also related to the wealth of society. At a time when society, when there are few, I would say, wealthy, burns what is possible, so it buys what is cheapest. There are quite large discrepancies between, for example. good Polish coal and coal imported from Ukraine. The pollination, of course, is increasing, but the price is also different. It also affects local pollination. There is no doubt that humans can also smoke waste, which also causes an increase in pollination. Ladies and gentlemen, there is no doubt that what is happening and what is being felt also depends on meteorological conditions. The smoke from the chimney looks different when it's hot winter, and it's been described many times, this smoke goes vertically up. Differently, when the weather changes, it comes to changing these harsh winters to more humid, heavy air. Especially this humid air with a large drop in temperature causes this smoke to lie on the surface of the earth. It also depends on how the agglomeration is located, the city. If it's in the nuisance, it's very large. If it is located above, on the high ground, where the air is blowing, the matter is quite different. Yet another matter is when the city is built properly. Why am I talking about this? Because this local pollination depends on the specific characteristics of individual cities. Indeed, on the one hand, this strategy is moving towards the state, as you said, towards ensuring the security of society, on the other hand towards self-government, so that the local government can act this way in order to create such conditions as to actually make this pollination as small as possible. Ladies and gentlemen, in order to understand the whole matter at all, I would like to return to certain roots. This is related to my political activity, namely the directive you are talking about here, as regards PM2,5, which was created in 2008. But it started... [[Telephone sound]] I'm so sorry. [[Member Beat Małecka-Libera: Can you turn that phone off?]] ...to be implemented in 2007 and then there was a discussion in the Council of Europe. At the time we were building a coalition, saying that this directive is extremely dangerous also for the Polish industry, and this is because pollination looks different in different places in Europe. [[Minister of the Environment illustrates his speech with slides]] You can look at the map you have here. This is the pollination of the average annual - this is how it is measured - for PM2.5 dusts on a European scale. If you'd seen this... Countries such as Spain, Portugal, coastal countries, the Atlantic Ocean, as well as Scandinavian countries have had average annual pollination almost eight times less than what is in the region where Poland is, where Germany is, where it is also, notice the country, Ukraine. So theoretically, if we were to consider only this, we could say, well, the difference in the culture of use or combustion. And where do these dusts come from? Yes, these dusts come from the fact that someone is burning something, but also from the industry. These dusts also come from low emissions when it comes to cars. It is difficult to say that in these countries there has been many times less consumption in the ocean, or that there has been a greater culture of cohabitation with the environment, that they have been less emitted. I think it's pretty clear what's on the ocean: the winds of the western winds are blowing rather clean air from the ocean, and if there's winds the other way, it's blown out into the ocean, it's coming back. These weather conditions play a major role. So what I'm saying is that this is also something that needs to be looked at from this point of view. When we discussed whether to introduce this directive and such strict standards, we said that Poland is a little at a disadvantage. Why? Because dust is not only what comes from anthropogenic energy sources or from the use of natural resources. This is also due to pollination, which is related e.g. with the use of soil. In Sahara, where there is no man, pollination is many times greater than in Europe because there are sharp winds and this dust also rises. [[Member Beat Małecka-Libera: What do we care about Sahara?]] It is this dust, of the same size, that is actually flew to other places, to Europe, also to Poland. Ladies and gentlemen! I'm talking about it because it's something you have to pay attention to. We've adopted certain standards, and I think these standards need to be adopted, since we've adopted them... And there was a strong discussion, including, among other things. with Mr Sigmar Gabriel, the Minister of the Environment, and we even had a coalition to prevent this draft directive from being passed, with these strict standards. But the fact is that at the moment, in the 27 countries of the world, Europe, I apologize very much, 23 countries in the European Union are out of line. It shall be conducted by the European Commission in accordance with Article. 13 of the Directive, checking procedures. Among these countries, 16 have already started procedures before the European Court of Justice, including Poland. Ladies and gentlemen! Now, as far as actions are concerned, these actions - as you have said - on the one hand they belong to you, and these are systemic, legal, technical, financial, organisational or social tasks, but there are also tasks that belong to local governments. Each country, as I said, has its own specific characteristics in this area. I think we will also be discussing this here. Ladies and gentlemen! The strategy for responsible development, which the Council of Ministers will adopt, I hope, during this week, is ready. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u199	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I present a report by the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications of the Government draft law amending the Penal Code Act and some other laws, the draft is included in print No 888. Marshal of the Sejm at the 29th meeting of the Sejm on 3 November 2016 pursuant to Article. 87. 2 in conjunction with Article. 90. 1 of the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm referred the above draft law to the Extraordinary Commission for amendments to the codifications for its consideration. The Extraordinary Commission has set up a permanent subcommittee on changes in criminal law. The permanent subcommittee shall be composed of five members. At the meeting of the three-member subcommittees, this position was taken that there was no support for the three-member subcommittee. One Member was in favour, one against, one abstention. This report was presented at the next Extraordinary Commission meeting on 12 January 2017. At this meeting, the Extraordinary Commission reviewed this proposal and introduced amendments. This presentation by the government is primarily a novelization of the article. 115 criminal code. Let me remind you that this is so-called. A dictionary of legal terms. The concept of significant damage is introduced in this project, i.e. when the value at the time of the act exceeds 200 thousand. zł. The concept of great damage is also introduced, i.e. when the value at the time of the act exceeds PLN 1 million. More stringent sanctions in the form of imprisonment have also been applied. This is intentional because of the extent of the damage that can be caused by the mass extortion of VAT. The concept of invoice, which is already legally known, has been accepted as there is a provision in Article. 2 point 31 of the Act of 11 March 2004 on tax on goods and services. The invoice shall be understood to mean a document in paper form or in electronic form containing the data required by the laws and regulations issued pursuant to it. Additional provisions have been introduced in this draft into part of the Criminal Code: Crimes against documents, namely Article. 270a § 1, 2 and 3. These prohibited acts, which we introduce, can all be committed only on purpose, i.e. with a direct intention or a possible intention. There is no criminalisation of such acts in the case of involuntary crimes. There is also a provision in the various articles concerning the so-called. a more stringent qualification. Where this is a damage caused in the property of great value, up to five times the value of this property, i.e. if it exceeds PLN 5 million, it is a threat of imprisonment from years 3, i.e. an act which in the language of criminal law constitutes a crime, but is also accepted a minor accident, then acts at risk of imprisonment up to years 2, in which case the provisions on conditional remission may be applied, provision may be made for exceptional relief or withdrawal from the penalty in the event of compliance with certain statutory conditions. There is also a provision for a further act prohibited in Article. 271a § 1, it concerns the perpetrator who issues the invoice or invoices, the total value of which is significant, i.e. more than 200 000. zł. There is also a qualified figure of this act, i.e. the case of the perpetrator who issues an invoice for an amount greater than five times the amount determining the value of the property, i.e. more than 5 million PLN. Also here is a minor accident. As regards the criminalisation of the next specific prohibited act, an Article is introduced. 277c § 1: Who shall commit the offences referred to in Article. 270a § 1 or 271a § 1 Penal Code - just mentioned by me - in relation to the invoice or when there is a total amount of charges, the value of which is greater than ten times the amount which determines the value of the property of great value, i.e. more than 10 million PLN, and this must be the value of this public debt, it cannot be confused with the value of the invoice, it is the value of the public debt, i.e. the amount of the tax. There have also been changes to the Police Act, the Border Guard Act, the Military Gendarmerie Act, the Central Anti-corruption Office Act and the National Tax Administration Act. These are changes resulting from the introduction of these new offences, namely those just mentioned by me, in the article. 270a, 271a and 277c. As far as these offences are concerned, law enforcement authorities will have the possibility to conduct so-called offences. operational control and the approval of operational operations must be given by a district court. Also during the second reading work, the date of entry into force of the Law was changed to 1 March 2017. On behalf of the Extraordinary Commission, I ask the High House to support this government submission, currently published in print No 1189. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u203	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Improving VAT collection and combating tax fraud is extremely important, but we will not be able to support this law for at least five reasons. First of all, I would like to remind you that on 1 January this year new VAT rules and amendments to the Tax Code have been introduced, which already significantly tighten the sanctions for understatement, for overpayment of VAT refunds, for issuing empty invoices. The mechanism for applying VAT reversed was introduced to a wider extent, changes were made to the registration and registration of VAT-axes, reduced quarterly VAT accounts. All measures to increase sanctions, including for crimes, multiple tax offences, are just starting to work and maybe it would be worth seeing how they work before we introduce further restrictions. Secondly, in this project the penalty is removed from the actual losses that occurred in the state budget. It is almost as if the purpose of this law is to make such a detachment happen. However, with such a detachment - and it is sometimes so that the value of the invoice itself is quite high, but because the rate was low, so also the loss was not so big, what's more, it may be that there was no reduction, but it was found that the invoice was wrongly displayed, falsified, wrong date, some other data - the penalty must occur. Thirdly, dualism is being introduced here in the proceedings against the taxpayer. On the one hand, his case will be the subject of proceedings by the tax office, before which he will have to explain himself, to be explained. And he may come to an agreement with this tax office in the sense that he will pay the penalty, pay the amount resulting from the correction, introduce a supplement. But all of this -- there might even be a waiver of the criminal case -- all of this doesn't change anything, and it doesn't tie prosecutors into criminal proceedings. Still, this entrepreneur whose case was even dismissed may be responsible to the prosecutor for somehow making the wrong invoice that something was wrong there. Fourthly, horrendous punishments are being introduced. Ladies and gentlemen, penalties are introduced to 25 years in prison, and the minimum penalty in many cases is 5 years in prison. These are more drastic punishments than mass rape with child cruelty. These are more drastic punishments than for overthrowing the constitutional authority of the Polish state. The National Judicial Council clearly states in its opinion that the introduction into the Criminal Code of so drastic penalties for financial-type offences that the introduction of them into legal order will make the system of criminal penalties internally inconsistent. However, the Penal Code has a hierarchy of goods that we protect and a hierarchy and a systematicisation of penalties. As drastic punishment as 25 years in prison would be some kind of disharmony in the Penal Code. There is talk of deliberate guilt, as the rapporteur was polite to note. This is because there are very many VAT rates, complicated rules, and sometimes in absolute good faith, but intentionally, you can enter a different rate, and then it turns out that the interpretation has changed, that there is a new interpretation, that there is a different assessment of a given service or a given product. We've had a lot of cases like this before. I would like to remind you, for example, of these famous recess cabinets, which for some time were subject to tax reduced as part of a building element, and at some point the tax offices began to interpret that it was a furniture that could be changed, pulled out, and, in fact, the rate of 23 percent was applied to this, and so many entrepreneurs were punished. This law does not lead to increased detection of crime, and this is ultimately the most important, not the very application of some very high penalties. For all these reasons, we believe that the introduction of these solutions is premature. It is underdeveloped, creates dualism and greatly increases uncertainty in the operation of entrepreneurs. He'll do more harm than good. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u211	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Freedom and Solidarnia Circle, I present my position on the report of the Extraordinary Commission on amendments to the codifications of the Government draft law amending the Penal Code Act and certain other laws, prints Nos 888 and 1189. Its appalling scale is clearly demonstrated by the data reported by the Supreme Audit Chamber. Against the placing on the market of invoices documenting fictitious activities presented to the High House in June The report showed that the value of the contested invoices increased from PLN 18.7 billion in 2013 to PLN 81.9 billion in 2015. Only in 2015 360 thousand were disclosed. such invoices, and their average value was approx. 227,000. zł. The measures taken so far by the government by Prime Minister Beata Szydło to seal the VAT collection and anti-fraud system have produced the desired effect. According to data from the Ministry of Finance for the period January to November 2016, VAT revenues were 7.4% higher compared to the same period in 2015, i.e. up to PLN 8.4 billion. However, this does not mean that the measures already implemented must be stopped. This is why we welcome the bill submitted, recalling, however, that in the case of crimes, not so much the threating penalty is important, but the effectiveness of prosecuting such offences by developing mechanisms used by the police, the prosecutor's office and the treasury. The introduction of a central invoice register is a good solution in this respect, which I have mentioned several times from this point of view. This proposal concerns the inclusion of criminal offences related to VAT fraud by specific rules of ordinary criminal law in the Penal Code, while respecting the possibility of qualifying acts in the event of their minor social harm as criminal acts in the Tax Code. Several amendments have been introduced in the course of the Extraordinary Commission's work on changes in codification, which should be assessed positively. First of all, we are talking about raising the amounts that determine the amount of the penalty. It is to serve this purpose to introduce a concept of value of property, which is five times higher than the concept of property of great value, i.e. more than PLN 5 million, in the case of a crime at risk of punishment for years 3 imprisonment - art. 1 point 2 of the Act - and 10 times the amount of property of great value, for a criminal offence punishable by punishment for 5 years or 25 years imprisonment - Article. 1 point 4 of the Act. As the Director of the Legislative Department of the Ministry of Justice, Mr Tomasz Darkowski, pointed out in the committee, it is necessary to separate all these invoices, which may have even been deliberately falsified, but which were done by ordinary, ordinary entrepreneurs who did not commit large-scale criminal activities, as was the case with the VAT mafia. It turned out that according to statistics, the largest VAT carousels were generated in the case of amounts exceeding PLN 5 million. It is equally appropriate to assess the addition of a provision introducing a case of minor importance to the said criminal provisions, which allows for the non-qualifying of an act committed as a crime where the amount entered on the invoice is multi-million, but the public debt appears to be relatively small. Here, too, the proposal that an act would have to be committed on purpose alone should be welcomed. At a later stage of the legislative work, we believe that it is necessary to consider using a Crown Witness in this area - that is what Mr Jerzy Jachnik said in his speech - so that it is not so that the testimony of the Crown Witness, often involved in this case, could be used in the criminal trial as the only evidence of the prosecution. The point is that the testimony of the Crown witness is proof, but at the operational stage, at the stage of police and prosecution proceedings. The fight against organised crime - because this is the way to treat VAT fraud by the Mafia on a large scale, which has a negative and significant impact on the state budget - requires [[♪ The bell ♪]] decisive action. Therefore, the Free and Solidarni Circle will support the bill submitted. Thank you very much. [[Applause]] [[President of the session includes Deputy Marshal Barbara Dolniak Sejm]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u229	Dear Marshal! High Izbo! The course of legislative work. Work on the draft law on the National Agricultural Support Centre and the draft law The provisions introducing the Act on the National Agricultural Support Centre were initiated after the appointment of the Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development of the Group for the optimisation of the functioning of agencies and other subordinate and supervised entities by the Minister of Agriculture and Rural Development on 28 June 2016. On 23 August 2016, the draft laws were submitted for public consultation and inter-ministerial arrangements. The draft laws were adopted at the meeting on 1 December 2016 by the Permanent Committee of the Council of Ministers and recommended for the meeting of the Council of Ministers, which adopted them at its meeting on 17 January 2017. The next day, the President of the Council of Ministers, Ms Beata Szydło, addressed both projects to the Sejm of the Republic of Poland. Main targets. The process of assessing the functioning of paying agencies in Poland, as well as the establishment on their basis of an institution implementing responsible development of agriculture and rural areas, is one of the main objectives to achieve, which are included in the programme of activities of the Ministry of Agriculture and Rural Development for the years 2015-2019. There are three agricultural agencies in the current state of law in the area of agriculture, i.e. Agricultural Restructuring and Modernisation Agency, Agricultural Market Agency and Agricultural Property Agency. The first two agencies, the first two institutions act as accredited paying agencies of the European Union. In social perception, this leads to opaqueness, duplication and blurring of competences and responsibilities in support of agriculture. The proposed regulations are part of the process of optimizing the functioning of agricultural agencies in Poland, which consists primarily of reducing the number of agricultural agencies operating in Poland, i.e. Arimr, ARR and Agricultural Property Agency, to one agency, i.e. The Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, and the creation of a unit responsible for the programming of Polish rural development and management of agricultural real estate remaining in the country's resources, i.e. the National Centre for Agricultural Support, secondly, leaving one paying agency: the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, which will be the only one to carry out the tasks of the paying agency of the European Union in the area of the Republic of Poland. It should be stressed that the proposed solution is part of the text of the Article. 7 of the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of 17 December 2013 whereby each Member State, having regard to its constitutional provisions, limits the number of accredited paying agencies to not more than one at national level. This is intended in particular to ensure greater transparency of national control systems and to reduce the organisational burden of administration and audit. Thirdly, the use of the resources of the Agricultural Market Agency and the Agricultural Property Agency, which are being abolished, to ensure the functioning of the National Agricultural Support Centre and the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture. Now I'm going to go to the National Agricultural Support Centre. The National Agricultural Support Centre, with its registered office in Warsaw, will be a state-owned legal entity which is an executive agency within the meaning of the Law of 27 August 2009 on public finances, supervised by the Minister responsible for rural development. The National Agricultural Support Centre will be headed by the Director-General appointed by the President of the Council of Ministers at the request of the Minister for Agriculture and Rural Development. The National Agricultural Support Centre will also be equipped with field structures corresponding to the administrative division of the country and operating at voivodship level. The basis for establishing the field structures will be their current counterparts to the Agricultural Property Agency and the Agricultural Market Agency, which are replicating. As a result, the economic aspects resulting from the consolidation of these structures are expected to be positive. This applies primarily to the cost of renting premises or numbers, e.g. executive positions. The legal basis for the action of the National Agricultural Support Centre will be, as in the case of other executive agencies, the statute given by the Minister responsible for rural development, which ensures that solutions can be flexibly adapted to the needs of current tasks while maintaining the priorities of the statutory framework. However, the arrangements currently in place in the Agricultural Market Agency and the Agricultural Property Agency remain unchanged in relation to the appointment and competence requirements of the person managing the National Agricultural Support Centre and its alternates, as well as those holding management positions where the appointment was maintained as the basis for establishing the employment relationship. At the same time, it is proposed to resolve this issue directly in the draft law in order to eliminate any doubts arising as to who is carrying out labour law activities with regard to the Director-General. As a general rule, the Minister responsible for rural development will carry out work in relation to the Director-General. The requirements for persons managing the National Agricultural Support Centre and their alternates, as well as for managers, are set out in the same way as in the case of the Agricultural Property Agency and the Agricultural Market Agency. The way in which workers are recruited is not modified in relation to the solutions in force in the agencies that have been abolished, I repeat, the way in which employees are recruited based on the principle of openness and competitiveness is not modified. The rules for the remuneration of workers will be laid down in the remuneration rules established by the Director-General of the National Agricultural Support Centre with the approval of the supervising NKR Minister, i.e. the Minister responsible for rural development. The Director-General and Branch Directors of the National Agricultural Support Centre shall be provided with the competence to issue administrative decisions within the framework of their tasks. This competence is necessary in order to carry out this part of the tasks of the National Agricultural Support Centre, which will be carried out under the legal regime. The financial economy of the National Agricultural Support Centre will be conducted on an equivalent basis to those of the agencies abolished, taking into account the need to combine their specific characteristics, in particular the need for the efficient management of the agricultural property of the Treasury, currently carried out by the Agricultural Property Agency. In financial terms, two separate financial plans for the Agricultural Property Agency and the Treasury's Agricultural Property Resources were an expression of this specificity. The aim of the project is that only the provisions on the financial management of the National Agricultural Support Centre will be included, while the provisions on the stock will remain in the provisions of the Act of 19 October 1991 on the management of agricultural real estate. The National Agricultural Support Centre will carry out the existing tasks of the Agricultural Property Agency resulting from the Act of 19 October 1991 on the Management of Agricultural Real Estate, such as the creation of family farms and the improvement of their area structure, the creation of conditions conducive to the rational exploitation of the productive potential of the Agricultural Property Resources of the State Treasury, the marketing of real estate and other assets of the State Treasury used for agricultural purposes, the initiation of institutional and agricultural works on the State Treasury's land and the promotion of the organisation of the State Treasury's private farms, the creation and exercise of rights and shares in shares in livestock farming and farming companies of particular importance for the national economy. The following tasks, which result from the well-known Act of 11 April 2003 on the shaping of the agricultural system, relate in particular to: improving the area structure of agricultural holdings, preventing excessive concentration of agricultural property, ensuring that agricultural activities are carried out on agricultural holdings by persons with appropriate qualifications. The following tasks result from the Act of 8 July 2005 on the exercise of rights to compensation for leaving property outside the current borders of the Republic of Poland for the transfer of part of the funds obtained from the sale of real estate and the lease of property of the Agricultural Property Resources of the State Treasury to finance the compensation fund. In addition, the National Agricultural Support Centre will take over 15 tasks of the Agricultural Market Agency and 12 tasks of the Agency will be delegated to the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture. In particular, the National Agricultural Support Centre will carry out the following additional tasks: monitoring the production of agricultural biogas, the market for bio-components and liquid biofuels, the production of bioethanol, the collection of information on the existing energy infrastructure under construction or planned for the production of electricity from agricultural biogas and the bio-components and liquid biofuels. The next task is to support agri-food promotion funds. The next task is to carry out promotion and information measures aimed at promoting agricultural and food products, with the exception of information and promotion measures for agriculture and food economy carried out by ministers responsible for agriculture, agricultural markets, fisheries or rural development. The next tasks derive from the Act of 12 March 2004 on Social Assistance as an intermediary in the management system of the operational programme under the European Fund for European Aid to the Most Deprived, referred to in the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of 11 March 2014 on the European Fund for Aid to the Most Deprived. It is also the task of participating in the programmes referred to in the Act of 11 July 2014 on the rules for the implementation of cohesion policy programmes financed in the financial perspective of 2014 and 2020 as an intermediate institution, an implementing institution or as a beneficiary. KOWR will also carry out delegated tasks from the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, among others. as regards the school fruit and vegetables scheme, the aid for the consumption of milk and milk products in educational establishments, the sugar market, including the diversification of the sugar industry, the monitoring of production and the levy on the sugar market, the processing of industrial sugar and the refining of sugar imported from outside the European Union, intervention purchases and sales of agricultural products on individual markets, including butter, skimmed milk powder, cereals and meat. A completely new task will be to support actions for renewable energy sources, in particular in agriculture. In addition, the Council of Ministers will be able to entrust the National Agricultural Support Centre with tasks other than those set out above relating to the functioning of agricultural and food markets. Ultimately, the National Agricultural Support Centre will also have competence in agricultural advice. The next issue I will discuss is the process of setting up the National Agricultural Support Centre. The process of setting up the National Agricultural Support Centre requires amendments to 43 laws. The amendments made to the laws governing the tasks of the agencies consist primarily of separating the existing tasks and competences of the agencies between the newly established National Agricultural Support Centre and, in particular, on the payment tasks of the common agricultural policy, the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture. As a general rule, proposals for legal solutions do not modify the current behaviour of the authorities in terms of their tasks, but only if they adapt this procedure to the organisational structures of the entities that will take over them. The draft Law Implementing the Act on the National Agricultural Support Centre indicates as the date of entry into force of the Act on the National Agricultural Support Centre on 1 July 2017 and regulates in detail the consequences of the abolition of the Agricultural Market Agency and the Agricultural Property Agency. The main effect of the abolition of these agencies is that they will lose their legal existence on 30 June 2017. However, it is not envisaged for this reason to conduct the process of winding up these agencies, as their tasks, as well as any rights and obligations are taken over by the Act respectively by the existing Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture and newly established on 1 July 2017. National Agricultural Support Centre. The draft law provides that the National Agricultural Support Centre shall, as of 1 July, by law, enter into the general rights and obligations of the Agricultural Market Agency and the Agricultural Property Agency, with the exception of the rights and obligations relating to the performance of tasks assumed by the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, i.e. purely payment tasks, which will become rights and obligations of that Agency as of 30 June 2017. This will exclude the risk of missing certain rights and obligations when transferring them to new entrants by indicating the overall success of the agencies. The proposed solution concerns all rights and obligations of the agencies, in particular their property, claims and obligations, as well as the replacement of the former Agricultural Market Agency and the Agricultural Property Agency, the existing agreements and agreements and the rights and obligations arising from the law, so decisions, provisions and other administrative acts. In this respect, it is particularly important that the National Agricultural Support Centre is the general successor of the Agricultural Property Agency. It shall enter into the rights arising from the existing Article. 5 of the Act of 19 October 1991 on the management of agricultural property of the State Treasury, and thus will be - as is currently the Agricultural Property Agency - a trustee of the State Treasury in respect of the property belonging to the agricultural property of the State Treasury. The National Agricultural Support Centre will therefore exercise property and other property rights in favour of the Treasury. With this solution, it will not be necessary to re-allocate the exercise of these rights to the new unit, that is, the National Agricultural Support Centre. Each agency currently operates on the basis of a financial plan, which is annexed to the State budget. It is therefore necessary to introduce instruments to modify these plans, as well as for the new unit, i.e. the National Agricultural Support Centre, to develop a new financial plan. It is therefore proposed to introduce an adjustment provision whereby the President of the Council of Ministers will be able to carry out, by means of a regulation, the transfer of planned budget revenue and expenditure, including salaries, between parts, divisions and chapters of the State budget, with the exception of public funds directly resulting from the Act. Plenipotentiary. This is the next moment I'm going to describe the proxy's actions. The draft legislation introducing the Act on the National Agricultural Support Centre provides that the Minister responsible for Rural Development shall appoint within 3 days of the day following the publication of the Act a representative for the establishment of the National Agricultural Support Centre. The purpose of the representative's action will be to organise a National Agricultural Support Centre, in particular: to prepare proposals in the field offices and offices of the National Agricultural Support Centre, to undertake activities related to the acquisition by the National Agricultural Support Centre and the Agency for the Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture rights and obligations, including the property of the Agricultural Property Agency and the Agricultural Market Agency, to undertake activities related to the regulation of labour relations with the Agricultural Property Agency and the Agricultural Market Agency's staff, to develop a draft financial plan of the National Agricultural Assistance Centre for the period from 1 July 2017 to 31 December 2017 and to submit it for approval to the Minister responsible for rural development, to undertake other activities necessary for the start of the activities of the National Agricultural Assistance Centre. The next topic I want to discuss is accreditation. It is also foreseen that, in the period preceding the essential date of entry into force of the Act, a number of other preparatory actions will have to be carried out in order to fully take over the agency's tasks of the Agency for the Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture and the National Agricultural Support Centre. In particular, it is necessary to take action to grant accreditation to the Agency for the Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture as a paying agency to the extent that accreditation currently held by the Agricultural Market Agency covers. It is therefore proposed to introduce a provision whereby the Minister responsible for public finances will carry out activities to grant, on the basis of the provisions on the financing of the common agricultural policy as from 1 July 2017, the Agency for the Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture temporary accreditation as a paying agency to the extent necessary to carry out the tasks of the paying agency which, as of the date of entry into force of the Act, will belong to the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture. And the President of that agency and the President of the Agricultural Market Agency will take action to obtain this accreditation by that date by the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture. Such regulation should enter into force on a date prior to the essential date of entry into force of the Act. It will also form the basis for public spending to take these measures. The next topic - everyone is important, this too - is employee matters. The draft Law, which introduces the Act on the National Agricultural Support Centre, provides that the Minister responsible for rural development shall establish, by way of order, the rules for the remuneration of the staff of the National Agricultural Support Centre. Given that the employment proposal at the National Agricultural Support Centre will be submitted to the staff of the Agricultural Market Agency and the Agricultural Property Agency by a proxy before the establishment of the National Agricultural Support Centre, it is clear that the rules for the remuneration of workers at the national centre will not yet apply. These rules will be adopted at the earliest on the date of establishment of the National Agricultural Support Centre. It is therefore necessary to lay down the rules according to which the agent will propose remuneration to the previous employees of the Agricultural Market Agency and the Agricultural Property Agency. It is necessary to issue this order in order to enable the proxy to propose to the staff of the two employment agencies at the National Agricultural Support Centre under certain conditions. The rules for the remuneration of workers in the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture are laid down in the rules for the remuneration of that agency, as I have already pointed out, and the employment proposal will take into account the conditions resulting from those rules. With regard to changes in the employment relationship, the proposed provisions provide for the provision of an appropriate proxy for the tasks to be carried out by the National Agricultural Support Centre, by the President of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture within the framework of the tasks assumed by that institution to propose the employment of workers of the agencies abolished in accordance with the lists submitted by the two Presidents, the Agricultural Market Agency and the Agricultural Property Agency, respectively, in the National Agricultural Support Centre or the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture under certain working and wage conditions. The employment proposal will be submitted by 31 March 2017. On the other hand, the employee will have one month to accept or refuse to accept the proposal. As a result of the adoption of the proposal, the former member of the Agricultural Market Agency or the Agricultural Property Agency will become an employee of the National Agricultural Support Centre or of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture, respectively, on 1 July 2017. If the employment proposal is refused, the employment relationship of these workers will expire on 30 June 2017. The same effect, i.e. the termination of the employment relationship, will take place in the event of failure to submit a declaration of acceptance or refusal to accept the employment proposal within the time limit and if, by 31 March 2017, the terms of employment proposed by the proxy or by the President of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture will not be communicated to the persons concerned by 31 March 2017. This is a specific way of putting an end to the agency's workers' employment relationship and it is therefore necessary to introduce rules guaranteeing certain benefits in this respect. It is proposed that these workers should be paid in cash under the rules laid down in the Act of 13 March 2003 on special rules for the termination of employment relationships with workers for reasons not affecting employees. The proposed mechanism will allow for the acquisition of a sufficient number of workers providing work at the Agricultural Market Agency and at the Agricultural Property Agency by institutions that will carry out these tasks after the date of entry into force of the Act, and will not jeopardise the continuity of these tasks. As a result of this regulation, the National Centre for Agricultural Support will count 1778 employees and the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture will count 11 302 workers. I will add here that from the Agricultural Market Agency to the National Agricultural Support Centre will pass - this is the plan - 483 jobs, 483 people, and from the Agricultural Market Agency to the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization is planned to move 322 employees. 'Cause that number could be shocking, 11,000. She was big because this is, if I remember correctly, 313 district units, so... they serve directly to farmers. The proposed rules also lay down the rules for the continuation of judicial and administrative proceedings following the entry into force of the new rules and indicate the authorities competent to amend, repeal, annul or terminate administrative decisions issued in respect of tasks adopted by the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture and the National Agricultural Support Centre, as well as the authorities competent to reopen proceedings completed before the date of entry into force of the Act. In view of the need to carry out transfers of information systems between agencies, expenditure for this purpose is expected to be included in the financial plans of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernisation of Agriculture and the National Agricultural Support Centre for the following years. The last section I'm going to discuss is about benefits. The draft law, the draft laws - because there are two, material and introductory - meet the systemic reform of agricultural agencies operating in Poland. Designed solutions eliminate and improve the negative social duality of agricultural agencies with primarily payment functions. Thus, payment functions carried out on behalf of the European Union and development, promotion and education functions are concentrated in one institution. The socio-economic effect will therefore be to increase the efficiency and efficiency of the Agency for Restructuring and Modernization of Agriculture and the National Centre for Agricultural Support while minimising costs. The implemented optimisation transformations and the establishment of a new institution - the National Centre for Agricultural Support - will bring tangible social benefits and benefits to the state budget through financial savings and the transfer of the saved funds to development activities, possible future tasks that the National Centre for Agricultural Support will be able to carry out. Focusing actions on priority objectives and meeting the objectives of a single point of contact of the beneficiary, see farmer, so-called. a single window and greater availability of support for beneficiaries. Finally, I inform the High House that the draft laws are compatible with European Union law. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u275	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present to you a draft law amending the Law on the Prevention of Pollution from Ships and some other laws. The need to develop a project and to revise the laws is mainly due to the need to implement and implement in Polish law certain provisions on maritime spatial planning, ship recycling, monitoring, reporting and verification of carbon dioxide emissions from maritime transport and sampling and reporting on sulphur content of marine fuels. This Act, in addition to the Act of 16 March 1995 on the Prevention of Pollution from Ships, amends the Act of 21 March 1991 on Maritime Areas of the Republic of Poland and Maritime Administration, the Law of 20 December 1996 on Ports and Seaports, the Act of 27 April 2001 on Environmental Protection, the Law of 18 August 2011 on Maritime Safety, the Law of 14 December 2012 on Waste, the Law of 24 July 2015 amending the Law on Maritime Safety and certain other laws and the Act of 5 August 2015 on Maritime Labour. Proposed amendments to the Law on the Prevention of Pollution from Ships concerning the so-called THETIS-EU will ensure the proper functioning of inspections on the basis of an IT system developed by the European Commission which allows the collection of data on ship inspections in the framework of the enforcement of the sulphur directive. In addition, the issue of sampling marine fuel from the fuel circulation system was clarified, sanctions for infringements of the sulphur directive were clarified, and the authority to which ship owners of Polish vessels would be able to apply for authorisation to test emission reduction methods for tests outside Polish maritime areas only in international waters was identified. The proposed amendments to the Law on the Maritime Areas of the Republic of Poland and the Maritime Administration take into account the competence of the Minister responsible for. energy in the process of issuing by the competent territorial directors of maritime offices permits the laying and maintenance of cables or pipelines in marine waters and territorial waters. In addition, as recommended by the European Commission, a definition of planning and spatial planning of marine areas of internal waters, the territorial sea and the exclusive economic zone has been added. The proposed amendments to the Law on Ports and Seaports by adding the obligation to provide fire protection in ports are of a sequencing nature. The proposed amendments to the Maritime Safety Act concern the regulation of the period of validity of qualification examinations and certificates of completion of training for seafarers and fishermen, the definition of the publication of the list of examiners of the Central Maritime Examination Commission, the indication of the duties of the shipper and the Container Terminal obligations resulting from Resolution MSC.380 (94) and the provisions of the SOLAS Convention, and the determination of penalties for non-compliance with the provisions on the obligation to weigh containers. An important amendment to the Maritime Safety Act is the implementation of new provisions forced by amendments to the SOLAS Convention concerning the need to verify the gross weight of containers loaded onto ships. These provisions aim to eliminate the underweight of containers in transport documents in relation to the actual weight of containers, resulting in serious maritime disasters. The proposed amendments to the Waste Act concern the regulation of ship recycling activities, including the obligation to obtain authorisation under an administrative decision. The draft law contains proposals for amendments to the Waste Act agreed with the Minister for the Environment concerning the regulation of ship recycling activities, including the obligation to obtain authorisation from the Marshal of the Voivodship under the administrative decision. As the tasks resulting from this regulation will be tasks of government administration commissioned to local government, the competent administrative authority in this area will be the Marshal of the voivodship. The proposed amendments to the Maritime Labour Act relate to placement and reimbursement of repatriation costs. The amendments result from the need to clarify at the level of the Act the characteristics of the directors of maritime offices as regards their new competences under the Maritime Labour Act. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u277	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I will present our opinion on the draft law amending the Law on the Prevention of Pollution from Ships and some other laws. As the Minister has already said, the purpose of the draft law amending the Law on the Prevention of Pollution from Ships and certain other laws is to implement certain provisions of the European Union's normative acts and international organisations facilitating the activities of maritime sector entities in the field of maritime spatial planning, ship recycling, monitoring, reporting and verification of carbon dioxide emissions from maritime transport and sampling and reporting on sulphur content of marine fuels. The project contains a number of references to the international legal acts mentioned in the explanatory memorandum due to the need to apply uniform legal and organisational arrangements to the entire system of supervision of the activities of maritime administrations. The bill, which the Minister also stressed, contains proposals for amendments to the Law on the Prevention of Pollution from Ships, the Law on Maritime Areas of the Republic of Poland and Maritime Administration, the Act on Ports and Seaports, the Act on Environmental Protection, the Law on Maritime Safety, the Law on Waste, the Act on the Amendment of the Law on Maritime Safety and the Law on Maritime Labour. The proposed solutions include the introduction of the IT system THETIS-EU, which will allow the collection of data on ship inspections in the framework of the enforcement of the sulphur directive and the Directive on port reception facilities for ship-generated waste and cargo residues, the introduction of a definition of planning and spatial planning of marine areas of internal waters, the territorial sea and the exclusive economic zone, the introduction of the period of validity of qualification examinations and certificates for the completion of the school for seafarers and fishermen and the publication of the list of examiners of the Central Maritime Examination Commission, the implementation of new provisions forced by amendments to the International Convention on the Safety of Life at Sea, the SOLAS Convention concerning the need to verify the gross mass of containers loaded onto ships - these provisions are designed to eliminate the under-weight of containers in transport documents in relation to the actual mass of containers, which has consequently caused dangerous marine disasters - and the regulation of ship recycling activities. Implementation into Polish law of current EU requirements will establish better conditions for the current control and reporting procedures related to ship inspections and will result in better supervision of compliance with legal requirements for the protection of the marine environment. The provisions of the draft law will also ensure that administrative authorities effectively carry out the tasks of supervising maritime operators. The proposed rules will not result in financial burdens on the state budget. Financial penalties for non-compliance with ship recycling requirements, the use on ships of non-compliant sulphur fuels and the non-compliance with monitoring and reporting obligations for CO2 emissions from maritime transport may generate additional revenue for the State budget. Similarly, additional revenue may be affected by checks on shippers carried out to approve the use of method two for verifying the gross weight of containers loaded onto ships to the State budget. Thanks to the proposed legislation, shipbuilding operators will be able to undertake a full and normal activity in the field of safe and environmentally sound ship recycling. This will certainly contribute to the development of this sector in our country by ensuring the conditions for ship recycling on competitive terms, while ensuring appropriate regulation. We welcome the draft presented and request that it be forwarded to the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u281	Thank you. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I have the honour to present a statement on behalf of the Kukiz Club15 on the government draft law amending the Law on the Prevention of Pollution from Ships and some other laws. We are all aware that this Act aims to implement some international documents into Polish law. According to any study on the Baltic Sea, pollution generated by ships contributes to the formation of excessive blooms and large amounts of sinews exceeding the balance of the Baltic Sea, which in turn adversely affects the purity of water, the amount of bacteria, the mortality of marine fauna and flora, causes the formation of surface goats, exhausts oxygen resources in water. The Baltic Sea is particularly sensitive to pollution. It is a relatively shallow water, a 56 m medium shelf sea, with a limited exchange of waters with the neighbouring North Sea, resulting in it being a reservoir with relatively low absorption capacity. Water circulation takes place only through the narrow Danish straits, so that the total exchange of salty Baltic waters into well-oxidised, high salty ocean waters lasts for 25 and 35 years. This means that all foreign substances brought into the Baltic Sea remain in it for many years, creating a huge threat to ecology and the entire water. That is why it is our point of view to ensure the best standard for the cleanliness of the waters of the Baltic Sea. The implementation of documents such as the update of the International Convention on the Safety of Life at Sea, the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council establishing a framework for maritime spatial planning, on ship recycling, monitoring, reporting and verification of carbon dioxide emissions from maritime transport introduces further Union tightenings. However, if not for them, we should still implement additional environmental regulations, including the Baltic, because it is in our vital Polish interest. The Act extends the list of definitions of the fuel system on board the ship. In 2000, the transport of oil in the Baltic waters amounted to over 80 million tons and in 2015 the transport mass of oil exceeded 130 million tons. There are dozens of fuel terminals operating on the Baltic Sea and their number is growing. These data indicate that, as oil transport increases by sea, the risks of the Baltic Sea ecosystem resulting from pollution of this reservoir of petroleum-derived compounds will also increase. We therefore consider it right that the proposed law introduces a definition of the fuel circulation system and the THETIS-EU system. THETIS-EU is intended to be a tool to improve the performance of inspection duties, including the selection of ships for inspection and the electronic reporting of its results. The position of the national administrator THETIS-EU and its tasks will be included in the Polish legislation. Another problem that is addressed by this Act is ship recycling. This is a growing challenge for shipowners from all over the world. Entrepreneurs, of course, want scrapping to be environmentally efficient. This problem has intensified in recent years due, inter alia, to the need to rejuvenate the fleet and adapt it to increasingly stricter requirements. It should be in the interest of the Polish state that recycling takes place ecologically, so that our ports, Szczecin, Gdynia, Tricity, and also, I do not know, Łeba, Ustka, are still clean. The Kukiz Posel Club15, recognising that the Baltic Sea is our national good, supports all actions that will lead to its protection. We therefore support this bill and we will be very much involved in its process in committee. Thank you.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u296	Thank you very much, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles.
