ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u297	Thank you, Marshal. I have the pleasure on behalf of the club Law and Justice to present our opinion on the draft law on investments in the construction of the water road connecting Zalev Wiślany with the Gulf of Gdansk, print no 1182. The aim of the bill is to improve the process of construction of the water road connecting Zalev Wiślany with the Gulf of Gdańsk implemented by the state budget under the multiannual programme established by Council of Ministers Resolution No 57/2016 of 24 May 2016. The construction of a waterway in the area of Polish marine waters under Polish jurisdiction is in line with the Polish state rhyme. This is the only way to ensure the free and undisturbed shipping of all flags to the port of Elbląg and other ports of the Vistula Bay. This investment is therefore of a priority nature and is implemented, as the applicant has put it, primarily because of the overriding public interest of ensuring the security and defence of the State. The water road connecting Vistula and Baltic Sea will first of all open new possibilities for evacuation, logistics, humanitarian aid and other elements of crisis management in the event of emergency situations. In addition, it will enable the development of the port of Elbląg and thus the maritime industry, tourism and logistics services. It will therefore contribute to economic development and to the decline in unemployment in the region. Unfortunately, experience has shown that the construction of roads and motorways and railway routes, in this case also of the sea road, requires the adoption of a special regulation, so-called. specifications to ensure that the investment can be implemented efficiently and in accordance with the timetable. The draft law therefore introduces procedural facilitations by simplifying the procedure for obtaining the necessary permits, including shortening the time limits resulting, inter alia. under the provisions of the Code of Administrative Procedure, Construction Law and Water Law. The Special Law also introduces sanctions that will be imposed on administrative authorities for failing to comply with the deadlines set out in the Act. It should be stressed that the construction of the waterway will be carried out in accordance with Polish and international environmental regulations. Any environmental impact issues, including impacts on Natura 2000 sites, will be resolved in the context of the environmental impact assessment in the framework of the decision on environmental conditions for investment in accordance with the current provisions of the Environmental Information and Protection Act, public participation in environmental protection and environmental impact assessments. The investment location option will also be selected during the environmental impact procedure. The draft law does not contain a decision in this respect. The provisions of the proposed law will be implemented primarily by the Director of the Maritime Office in Gdynia, who, as an investor, will carry out the investment, the Pomeranian Voivodeship, which, in agreement with the Warmia-Mazury Voivodeship, will issue a decision on the permission to implement the investment, and the Regional Environmental Director in Olsztyn, who, after consulting the Regional Environment Director in Gdańsk, will issue a decision on the environmental conditions for the investment. The procedure for issuing the investment decision and the content of the investment decision will be regulated in detail in the project. The decision will at the same time entitle the Director of the Gdynia Maritime Office to carry out works and will constitute a legal title to the ground. In addition, the issues of ground expropriation and the associated compensation scheme and rules have been specifically regulated. The adoption of the bill will allow the completion of administrative procedures in 2017 and the start of investment in early 2018. We therefore welcome the draft presented and request that it be forwarded to the Committee on Maritime Affairs and Inland Navigation. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u333	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, On behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party, I have today the honour and pleasure to present to the High House a draft law on the Councils and Councils and on the amendment of certain other laws. The project is intended to strengthen the institutional position of the Councils and bodies of the Councils, including the Councils themselves. The Chief's institution has been operating in our political and legal system since the Middle Ages. This office is one of the most characteristic components of organised life of settled rural communities. In these communities, beside the voluntary fire department, beside the parish or the rural hostels' circles, it is the council that is one of the most important and most needed inhabitants of the institution. The council not only represents the village to local authorities, it not only informs the residents of the announcements. The Council is usually the first to respond to the needs of its neighbours, to organize a collection of gifts for the firefighters in many tragic fire situations, and to organise aid for neighbouring villages affected by various difficult situations. He's just available every day and helpful. Most often, the Council is the initiator of changes and investments improving the daily existence of rural residents. The situation has improved significantly since the introduction of the Social Funds and the availability of European funds for rural development. The Chief may have more today than he could have done 10 or 20 years ago. The Chiefs take advantage of these opportunities very well. Thanks to these conditions that we managed to create, for example in the years 2007 to 2015, today the Polish village is developing rapidly and in many places it is visible to the naked eye as well as in this external dimension it is beautiful. But we want to move on. Councillors in our country continue to develop their small homelands. According to Art. Article 15 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 of the Act of 2 April 1997, i.e. the Constitution of the Republic of Poland, the territorial system of the Republic of Poland ensures the decentralisation of the public authority. This means that our system is built on the principle of subsidiarity, thus transferring as many competences to local communities as possible. We are convinced that the strength of the state comes from the attachment of citizens to the very small communities in which they live. It is obvious that the natural characteristic of man is to take care of the nearest environment in which he lives and works. In Poland, the smallest organizational unit in which people live is the village created on the basis of the Act of 8 March 1990 on local government. The villagers should have the right to actively influence the functioning and transformation of the local environment. Today, we want to give even greater empowerment to this smallest cell of the local government, which is the commonwealth. The current rules are not fully regulated, in our opinion, by the duties of the Chief and are underestimating his position. The functions and powers of the Soviet Councils, as well as of the rural meetings, are not fully defined. The tasks performed by the Chief shall be public tasks. In view of this, the proposed law in Article. Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 sets out that the municipality council shall lay down the rules on which the Councils and members of the Councils will be entitled to a diet or reimbursement of travel expenses. In addition, in Article 2, paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: Article 5 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 2 of the project is foreseen that the Heads, regardless of their diet and reimbursement of their travel expenses, will be remunerated in respect of a function of half the minimum salary, determined on the basis of the Law on Minimum Compensation for Work, for each month commenced. In addition, the term of office of the Chief will be equivalent to joining the pension insurance scheme and the remuneration referred to above will therefore be added to the establishment of the pension base. This, I think, is certainly the expected decision for those chiefs who have devoted most of their lives to this role, and such cases are not lacking, when the term of office of the chief is often 15, 20 or more years, and there are record-makers who perform this function much longer. High Izbo! It is important to appreciate the contribution of the Chiefs to our social life. This was our idea when we offered half of the minimum wage paid to the Councils constantly from the state budget. Currently, the effort of the chiefs is appreciated or not appreciated by local authorities. I know such cases myself, that the council of one municipality is properly rewarded, and his neighbour who lives in the neighbouring community gets nothing, and they are also chiefs who put many hearts into their work, in animating the activities of the local community. On this occasion, I want to calm down, dispel the concerns of local governments that arise here and there. In view of the fact that the new benefit will be paid from the State budget, it will not in any way burden either the local authorities or the municipalities, nor will it interfere with the exercise of local seats by the Councils, which a large group has also devoted themselves to doing so by working in the local government. According to the 2014 Statistical Yearbook of Voivodships, the number of heads is 40 382. Taking into account the flat-rate remuneration associated with their function, which amounts to half of the minimum monthly salary and the minimum wage for work, the costs that will be charged to the state budget will amount to PLN 40 million per month, i.e. over PLN 400 million per year. According to the project, the Council thanks to the new competences enshrined in the article. Article 4 (4) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1306/2013 shall apply. 1 of the project will be able to participate in the deliberations of the municipal council without voting rights. It will also benefit from the legal protection available to public officials. And here I will point out that this postulate has been signalled by the chiefs for many years. Some have tried to create parallel associations, but why complicate life when it can successfully reach for funds that are dedicated to infrastructure projects. So far, there has been a problem in the countryside: we would have done something positive for our village, but from somewhere we would have to raise money. Often the municipality, not very rich, was unable to support its ideas. To get the money, you had to start associations first, as I said. Only a small percentage of the village today has such associations and benefits from the opportunities available to these organisations. By making official organizations of public benefit, we will give everyone more than 40,000. a status that will allow greater flexibility when it comes to reaching for external resources. This is also in our view of far-reaching change. As we see today and praise the achievements of the 25th anniversary of Polish self-government, so in the future we will appreciate the achievements of strong, legal entities. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u381	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Although I have not heard the question to myself, to the Ministry, but I will use this moment to present our position, the position of the Ministry, because there is not yet a government position on this project. This project, as it has already sounded many times, assumes several solutions. Unfortunately, these solutions in our assessment raise great doubts. First of all, Art. 2 draft law on the Councils and Councils and on the amendment of certain other laws clearly goes beyond the framework set out in the Constitution of the Republic of Poland and the European Charter of Territorial Self-Government, because it equips only one type of auxiliary unit, the Council, with attributes specific to local self-government units, forgetting, for example. The neighborhood. It is also worth noting that granting the state the status of a local government unit would require further changes, namely the establishment of a local government office, the granting of budgetary and financial autonomy to them, the inclusion of the Councils in anti-corruption regulations, and the supervision of voivodeships and regional accounting chambers themselves. These regulations are not in this project. The project is inconsistent. Despite the project's independence, it is proposed that its service - both administrative and organisational as well as financial - should continue to run the municipality, so nothing would change. It is also questionable to propose the inclusion of villages in the catalogue of bodies entitled to carry out public service activities. This would lead to a reduction in the transparency of public transfers and to the preservation, properly shaken, of equal opportunities for applicants. Another reservation raises the principle of financing and employing the Heads of State proposed in the draft. The proposal that the remuneration of the Councils should be financed by the state budget raises quite a number of concerns because all other local authorities, their representatives, are financed from the budgets of these units. It seems doubtful, hence our reservations. However, it has not been defined at the same time how the employment relationship will be dealt with with by the Chief, how he will respond. In principle, there is no information on how to raise this 500 million for wages. Appreciating the role played by the Councils and the Councils in local communities and all activities contributing to the creation of civil society, it is unfortunately impossible to overlook the shortcomings of this project, hence the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration gives a negative opinion on this project. Thank you. [[Game in the O.R.]]

We proceed with the examination of item 22 of the agenda: the draft resolution presented by the Sejm Bureau on the commemoration of General Mariusz Zaruski on the 150th anniversary of his birth (print No 1224). I'll read the draft resolution. [[The Gathered Rise Up]] The resolution of the Polish Sejm on the commemoration of General Mariusz Zaruski on the 150th anniversary of his birth. On 31 January marks the 150th anniversary of the birth of General Mariusz Zaruski, an outstanding soldier and fighter for Poland's independence, as well as a sailor, tavern, prosaic and poet. Mariusz Zaruski was born in Dumanov, Podol. In his youth he made a number of deep sea voyages. He was sent to Archangelsk in 1894 for his participation in the conspiracy of independence in Odessa. At the beginning of the 20th century in Zakopane he founded the Ski Section of the Tatra Society and in 1909. The Tatra Volunteer Emergency Service he led. He also made numerous first climbs to the mountain peaks. Mariusz Zaruski was active in the Union of Active Fighting. In August 1914 he joined the firing squad of Józef Piłsudski, fought in the ranks of the Polish Legions as an officer in the 1st Ulysses regiment. He then participated in the conspiracy within the Polish Military Organisation. After regaining independence he served in the Polish Army. As commander of the 11th Ulysses, he fought against the Bolsheviks at the front of the war. He was awarded the Order of Virtuti Militari. In 1924, he was appointed Brigadier General. After his retirement he worked in Gdańsk Pomerania in Yacht - Club. He was president of the Polish Sailing Union and captain of the Scout Schooner 'Zawisza Czarny'. In December 1939 he was arrested by the Soviets in Lviv, he was imprisoned in Kherson, where he died in April 1941 in a prison hospital. The life and activities of Mariusz Zaruski, his steadfast attitude and ardent patriotism, great merits for sailing and tathering are a role model for future generations of Poles. The Sejm of the Republic of Poland recognises that General Mariusz Zaruski deserves his country well. I ask Members to adopt this resolution by acclamation. [[Applause]] Thank you very much. I state that the Sejm adopted a resolution on the commemoration of General Mariusz Zaruski on the 150th anniversary of his birth. High Seym! One more memorial. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u476	Thank you very much. Mr Tomasz Kostusz, Civic Platform, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u477	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! This week, as every year on January 26, we celebrate the Polish Transplant Day. This festival was established on the anniversary of the first successful kidney transplant in Poland, which was held at the Clinic of the Surgical Medical Academy in Warsaw in 1966. It was then 621. such a procedure in the world. The primary problem of Polish transplantology is too few organs to be transplanted in relation to the number of people who are expecting transplants. Hospitals show varying activity when it comes to reporting potential donors. It is estimated that at national level it is only about 2% all the dead. To increase the chance of waiting for transplants, hospitals should increase their activity in the process of identifying potential donors. A report on organ availability for transplantation, which outlines the multi-stage nature of transplant procedures, reveals a number of information. The main conclusion of the report shows how much more to do. Transplantology in Poland as a field of medicine is very well developed and controlled. It is crucial for the matter to prepare medical personnel for proper conduct in order to confirm death and to inform competently about the death of a loved one and the opportunity for another to obtain an organ from a deceased person. It is equally important to place increased emphasis on educational activities in this area. Each organ transplant has its source in a decision of great ethical value, a decision to selflessly sacrifice a part of its own body for the sake of the health and welfare of another human being. That is the nobleness of this act, which is an authentic act of love. These are the words of the great Pole, the Pope, St. John Paul II, who described organ donation to save human life. That is why I am appealing today to the ladies and gentlemen of Members, and also through you, through you, to all the people of Poland, for involvement and promotion of the idea of transplantology. An excellent example of commitment is the fulfilment of a declaration of will expressing the consent of the person who has such a document to receive organs from it. I would like to stress that a declaration of will is only an information print and its signature should be a reason for discussion among family, friends, friends, to express their will and their willingness to give organs after death. Therefore, by promoting the noble idea of saving lives by handing over organs to transplants, I encourage you to sign a declaration of will that can be downloaded today in many places on the Internet. In addition, on the occasion, I ask the Prime Minister and the Minister of Health to place greater emphasis on the issue of transplantology. It is impossible to ignore the fact that the seventh year of the National Programme for the Development of Transplant Medicine, established in the years of the Civil Platform's rule, has just begun. The government of Donald Tusk has established a programme in which over the next 10 years a total of PLN 45 million will be spent annually on transplant activities. I would like to ask Minister Radziwiłłła to present a report on the functioning of the programme with particular attention to information on how these funds are available and extensive information on future prospects by the end of 2020. From this point on, I would like to sincerely thank all doctors, nurses and all medical staff who take care of patients and their families. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u481	Mr. Marshal! I dedicate my statement to the feast of Gdansk Starogard, Pomerania. Dear Pomerania people! Dear citizens of the Old Gdansk Gdansk! On January 29, 1920 the town of Starogard was remembered as very cold. However, this did not disturb the crowds of the people of Starogard, who had long-awaited freedom after 148 years of Prussian rule. The atmosphere of those events connected with the arrival of Polish troops under General Józef Haller was very hot. After almost one-and-a-half years of captivity, Polish speech was again heard on the streets, smiles on the faces of the inhabitants, and tears of emotion flowing down the cheeks. Ladies and gentlemen, after the end of World War I, after the military defeat of the central states, a new political order has been established in Europe after the fall of Tsar Russia at the peace conference in Versailles. An extremely important accent of these talks was the message of President Wilson of the United States, one of 14 points of which was the issue of rebirth of the Polish state within its ethnic borders with free access to the sea. The Polish National Committee played a very important role in discussions on Poland's rebirth with its leading politicians: Roman Dmowski and outstanding pianist, later Prime Minister Ignacy Paderewski at the head. Under the provisions of the Treaty of Versailles, Poland was reborn by the Pomeranian Corridor, which connected Polish lands with the sea. Starogard was also in the corridor territory. The Pomerania population was expecting rapid implementation of the Treaty provisions. The executioner of these provisions turned out to be a gene. Józef Haller, who traveled through towns and towns in the Pomerania to implement these provisions. For his great day Starogard waited until that cold January day, when Polish troops entered the city through triumphal gates, in order to restore them to Poland forever on behalf of the Republic of Poland. Then came the time of submission to our public life in many areas. Ladies and gentlemen, let us keep the atmosphere of those events in our memory and in our hearts. May January 29th always we associate ourselves with regaining freedom and pride in being Poles. Let us also be aware that freedom, as the history of Europe or even the world shows, is quite rare in the full sense of this word, it can be said that it is a deficit. If we go back a dozen or dozen years, we need to see ourselves as witnesses of a wave of freedom passing through different countries, including the countries of our region. This freedom is opposed to various forms of resistance which oppose this freedom. It's proof of how much we have to resist it, as at all costs of freedom that wasn't given to us for free, we have to defend ourselves. The experiences of recent years clearly show that there is no freedom, including this national one, without a strong state. This area of freedom and democracy is provided by a strong national state. Therefore, anti-state attitudes deserve strong rejection, which has happened recently in our homeland. I believe that our nation has all the characteristics of a nation that is not only predisposed to attain freedom in full scope, but also has a great desire and love for freedom. Thank you. ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u484	Thank you. Mr Bogusław Sonik, Civic Platform, has the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u485	January 31st is the 150th anniversary of the birth of General Brig. Mariusz Zaruski. He was born in 1867 in Dumanov, Podol. He was a very talented man: a poet, a prosaic, a painter, a photographer, a traveler, a tavern and a sailor - a real Renaissance man, you can say. He loved the mountains and the sea, which translated into his public ministry. He was the creator of the Tatra Volunteer Rescue Service and the President of the Polish Sailing Union. Thanks to his patriotic upbringing, which he carried out from his family home, he served Poland throughout his life. In his youth he made many deep sea voyages. For his independence activity he was sent by the Tsarist authorities to Archangielska in 1894. After returning to Poland, he graduated from the Academy of Fine Arts in Krakow, and then moved to Zakopane to set up the Tatra Volunteer Emergency Service there in 1909. With the outbreak of the Great War, World War I, he became a legionary. After regaining independence, he served in the Polish Army as commander of the 11th Ulysses regiment. He was also an aide to President Stanisław Wojciechowski. He retired before the fall of May. As a retired general, Zaruski decided to devote himself to sailing, he was president of the Polish Sailing Union. He was arrested in 1939 by the NKVD in Lviv and then in prison in Kherson, where in 1941 he died of a disease called cholera. His patriotism and his love of his homeland, his work for the common good and his educational work are worthy of imitation. Therefore, commemorating the 150th anniversary of the birth of General Mariusz Zaruski is a form of thanking the Polish parliament for this outstanding Pole.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u486	Thank you. Mr Waldemar Andzel, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u487	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Law and Justice is preparing a proposal for changes in electoral ordination to local government. The most important demand is the introduction of two cadences of the mayors, mayors and presidents. A simultaneous extension of the term of office to 5 years is considered. This is a really great idea. Many mayors, the mayor of the city, have been in office continuously since the first local elections. It even happens that some have been in office for decades, because the former chiefs of the communes have become their uncles. This situation strongly restricts local democracy, consolidates different political agreements and promotes corruption. The participation of residents in the exercise of power must be increased and the structure of local authority itself, thanks to the changes introduced, would become more transparent. It will be impossible to create personal links and permanent, more or less official arrangements. Every mayor, mayor and president during the two term of office - because there are so many proposed changes - is able to do a lot for the local community. At that time you can present your vision and realize even these big projects. The mere fact that he will not be able to perform his duties by successive terms does not mean that he will disappear completely from public life. His experience will continue to be very valuable, and he himself will be able to continue serving the inhabitants of his city or commune, as well as being in the authorities of the county or the authorities of the voivodship. Other changes that are possible and which would undoubtedly make it difficult or even prevent any abuse are transparent urns and webcams in every polling place, new ways of counting votes, in which all committee members would have to take part, as well as the need to store ballots for several years. Law and Justice wants all these changes to be in force during the 2018 local elections. In my opinion, one more change is needed in the future, namely the liquidation of one-mandate electoral districts, i.e. the so-called. The JOWs. They are not the optimal solution for our political system. Some of the smallest municipalities had to be divided into districts that really counted as many as dozens of voters. The dividing lines are often artificial, and where the population is changing intensively, they will often have to be moved. In the case of larger municipalities, the problem is even more serious. The JOWs lead to a phenomenon where the best chance of obtaining a mandate is for those who run from the most famous committees, e.g. those who are focused around the current ruler, the mayor or the president. Many residents do not have any representation on the council. In addition, councillors elected in the JOWs may be disloyal to their committee and often do so. Representing a small district, they are attached to it, and in some situations the most important thing for them is the interest of one district, not the whole city, and the councilman should look at the interests of the entire community. I hope that all the new, better rules will be implemented and will be in force during the forthcoming local elections. Because although there is no system that is 100% flawless, one should strive for a form that is optimal, the most equitable and consistent with democratic standards. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u489	Message statement on farewell to the veteran Janusz Brochwicz-Lewiński ps. A griffin. Mr. Marshal! High Seym! Yesterday we said goodbye to Janusz Brochwicz-Lewiński's dog. Griffin, a Batalion soldier AK Parasol, commander of the defence of Michl's palace during the Warsaw Uprising. Janusz Brochwicz-Lewiński was born on 17 September 1920 in Wołkowysk. In September 1939 he fought as a corporal. After the aggression of the Soviet Union against Poland, he was captured by the Soviet Union on 18 September, from which he escaped and joined the ZWZ-AK. He served among others. Under the command por. Jeronim Dekutowskiego, one of the most famous soldiers of the anti-communist underground. From 1940 to 1942, he worked in Puławy as an administrative officer while working for Polish intelligence. The deconspirator entered Lublin and Janów Lubelski. Until January 1944 he led one of the guerrilla troops in Lublin. Among the Germans he had a nickname for the Knight Commander. Then he was transferred to the battalion for special tasks of AK Parasol, where he was an instructor in training in the conspiracy podchożnik. Shortly before the uprising, Brochwicz-Lewiński commanded an attack on Wendy's pharmacy near Krakowskie Przedmieście, during which tools, medications and dressing materials were acquired for underground soldiers. In the Warsaw Uprising until August 5, he commanded the defence of Michl's palace on Wola. His crew repeatedly resisted German assaults supported by armored craft. The Griffin was badly wounded in battle at the Evangelical cemetery in Wola. After the surrender, he was a prisoner of the POW camp. After the war, he was in exile in England. He joined the British Army. In 2002 he returned to Poland. He acted actively in the combatant environment, popularized history among young people. He supported the work of the Institute of National Remembrance to identify the victims of communist terror. IPN granted him the title of Custos of National Remembrance in 2016. In 2007, he participated in the billboard action of the Tomorrow Patriotism programme. Between 2009 and 2014 he was in the Chapter of the Order of the Virtuti Militari War Order. In 2014, he was a member of the Honorary Committee of the Link Foundation. Praise the Heroes!

ParlaMint-PL_2017-01-26-sejm-34-4.u491	Statement on the National Transplant Day. Dear Marshal, High Izbo! My statement today concerns a relatively young field of medicine, which is transplantology. Each year on January 26th we celebrate the Polish Transplant Day. The date did not come from the case, because the first successful transplant in Poland took place on that day in 1966 and it was a kidney transplant. It was then 621. such a procedure in the world. The first documented in the history of successful transplantation was performed in Boston in 1954, a kidney transplanted from a twin patient's brother. The pioneer of modern transplantology was French surgeon Alexis Carrel living at the turn of the 19th and 20th centuries. He experimented with transplanting organs to animals. His work allowed, among others. to develop suture technique of blood vessels. He also won the Nobel Prize. Other important dates in transplantology are: the first successful liver transplant in 1967 in Denver, the same year the heart was transplanted in Cape Town, and the first heart and lung transplant in Stanford was performed in 1981. Given the historical features in Poland, it should be mentioned that the first successful kidney transplants from the deceased were performed by professors Jan Nielubowicz and Witold Orłowski in Warsaw in 1966, while a successful heart transplant was performed by Zbigniew Religion in Zabrze in 1985. The first successful liver transplant was carried out in 1990 and in 1994 in an adult. Organ transplantation is a way to save lives. It is undoubtedly a miracle of medicine, for many human lives have been saved by it. Transplant procedures are not only organ procurement and transplantation, but also what happens to the patient before and after. I think today is the perfect time to be able to thank all those who promote this noble idea of saving lives by declaring organ donation for transplantation. Thank you very much.
